T^{\prime}=T^{\prime}(\|wg_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}})=T^{\prime}(\|wf_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}})=T^{\prime}(\delta_{3})
.
Furthermore, the loss of the time-decaying velocity weight
e^{(\theta-t)|v|^{2}}
arises from the following estimate of the nonlinear term:

\displaystyle e^{(\theta-t)|v|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\Gamma_{\text{gain}}(g,g)\mathrm{d}s\lesssim o(1)\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|e^{(\theta-s)|v|^{2}}g(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\Big)^{2}.

Second, we justify the favorable sign condition (
8.53
) by investigating the perturbation equation. Specifically, we introduce the iterative sequence

\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}:=e^{\phi_{E}/2}g^{m+1}-e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\sqrt{\mu},

satisfying that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(8.54)

\begin{cases}&\partial_{t}\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}-\nabla_{x}(\phi^{m}+\phi_{E})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}+\frac{v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{m}}{2}\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}+\nu^{m}\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}\\
&=e^{-\phi_{E}}K\mathfrak{f}^{m}-e^{-\phi_{E}/2}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{m}\sqrt{\mu}+e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\Gamma_{\text{gain}}(\mathfrak{f}^{m},\mathfrak{f}^{m}),\\
&\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}|_{\gamma_{-}}=f_{b},\quad\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}(0,x,v)=\frac{F_{0}-e^{-\phi_{E}}\mu}{e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\sqrt{\mu}},\\
&-\Delta_{x}\phi^{m}=e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathfrak{f}^{m}\sqrt{\mu}\mathrm{d}v\text{ in }\Omega,\quad\phi^{m}=0\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where
\nu^{m}:=e^{-\phi_{E}}\nu(v)+e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\nu(\sqrt{\mu}\mathfrak{f}^{m})
.
We now prove that

(8.55)

\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\ll 1.

Using the initial condition of
\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}
, together with the assumption
\|wf_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<\delta_{3}\ll 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}(0,x,v)=h+f_{0},\qquad\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m+1}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\ll 1.

We now apply the same characteristic argument as in
[
2
]
to (
8.54
). The source terms on the right-hand side of (
8.54
) are controlled by Lemma
12
and Lemma
9
:

(8.56)

\begin{split}\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\frac{\nu(v)}{2}(t-s)}[K\mathfrak{f}^{m}+\Gamma_{\text{gain}}(\mathfrak{f}^{m},\mathfrak{f}^{m})]\mathrm{d}s&\lesssim T^{\prime}\sup_{t\leq T}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}+T^{\prime}\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\\
\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\nu(v)}{2}(t-s)}|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{m}\sqrt{\mu}|\mathrm{d}s&\lesssim T^{\prime}\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim T^{\prime}\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

Together with the smallness assumption on the boundary term
|wf_{b}|_{L^{\infty}_{\partial\Omega,v}}\ll 1
, we conclude the following uniform-in-
m
estimate:

\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|w\mathfrak{f}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+C|wf_{b}|_{L^{\infty}_{\partial\Omega,v}}\leq C[\delta_{3}+|wf_{b}|_{L^{\infty}_{\partial\Omega,v}}].

Since
C[\delta_{3}+|wf_{b}|_{L^{\infty}_{\partial\Omega,v}}]\ll 1
, it follows that
\nu^{m}\geq\frac{\nu(v)}{2}
and we conclude (
8.55
).
Moreover, applying Theorem
1
, we derive that

\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\sup_{t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w\mathfrak{f}^{m}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\ll 1.

Consequently, the favorable sign condition (
8.53
) holds.

Third, we prove the existence and uniqueness of the solution to (
8.49
).
From the sign condition (
8.53
) and the initial assumption (
8.50
), we apply the same argument as in
[
2
]
to obtain the following uniform-in-
m
regularity estimate: for some
c>0
and
C_{6}>1
,

(8.57)

\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}e^{-c\nu t}\alpha_{g}^{m-1}\partial_{x,v}g^{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|e^{-c\nu t}\nabla_{v}g^{m}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{1+\delta}_{v}}<C_{6}C_{5},

where the dynamical kinetic weight
\alpha_{g}^{m-1}(t,x,v)
is defined in (
1.18
), with the self-consistent electric field

E_{g}^{m-1}(t,x):=-\big[\nabla_{x}\phi_{g}^{m-1}(t,x)+\nabla_{x}\phi_{E}(x)\big].

Using (
8.57
) and following the local well-posedness argument in
[
2
]
, we conclude that

g^{m}\to g\ \text{ in }L^{\infty}((0,T^{\prime});L^{1+}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))\ \text{ as }m\to\infty,

where
g
satisfies (
8.49
).
Moreover, since
g^{m}=e^{-\phi_{E}}\sqrt{\mu}+e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\mathfrak{f}^{m}
, we derive that

\mathfrak{f}^{m}\to\mathfrak{f}\ \text{ in }L^{\infty}((0,T^{\prime});L^{1+}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))\ \text{ as }m\to\infty,

where
\mathfrak{f}
satisfies (
8.17
).
The uniqueness of solutions to (
8.17
) is guaranteed in the space
L^{1+}_{x,v}
under the additional assumption
\|\nabla_{v}g_{0}\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}<\infty
; see
[
2
,
3
]
.
From (
8.55
) and the relation
g=e^{-\phi_{E}}\sqrt{\mu}+e^{-\phi_{E}/2}\mathfrak{f}
, we obtain the estimate for
\|wg\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
as in (
8.51
), without the time-decaying velocity weight
e^{(\theta-t)|v|^{2}}
.

Fourth, we establish the regularity estimate (
8.51
).
We work on the perturbation equation (
8.54
). Since
T^{\prime}\ll 1
, we can follow a simpler argument than in Proposition
20
, where the term
\mathcal{D}(t,x,v)
in (
8.32
) can be directly estimated using the smallness of the time interval
[0,T^{\prime}]
. The remaining terms can be treated as in Proposition
20
, so that we obtain the following estimates:

\displaystyle\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}(v)\eqref{hchara:K_nabla}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim T^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}^{m}(s)\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}},

\displaystyle\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}(v)\alpha_{f}^{m-1}(t,x,v)\eqref{hchara:K_nabla}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim T^{\prime}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{f}^{m-1}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}^{m}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},

where
\alpha_{f}^{m-1}:=\alpha_{g}^{m-1}
.
Using the initial condition (
1.19
), we obtain a uniform-in-
m
estimate for (
8.54
):

\displaystyle\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}^{m}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{f}^{m-1}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}^{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{f}^{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{5}\delta_{2}.

Since
\mathfrak{f}^{m}\to\mathfrak{f}
strongly, we derive

\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{f}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{f}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{6}\delta_{2}.

This further implies that

\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}g\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\sup_{m}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{6}C_{5}.

Finally, we prove the continuity of
\|wg(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
in time
t
, and the positivity of the solution.
To prove the continuity, it suffices to prove that
w\mathfrak{f}
,
\alpha_{f}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}
, and
\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{f}
are continuous in time
t
.
Indeed, these three terms are represented via the Duhamel formula along characteristics, as in (
8.56
) and Proposition
20
. The continuity then follows by comparing the representations at times
t
and
t-\delta
and letting
\delta\to 0
, which yields continuity in time. For a similar argument, we refer to Step 9 in Theorem 2 of
[
3
]
.
The unique solution
F=\sqrt{\mu}g
is non-negative since (
8.52
) preserves positivity.
∎

Remark 8
.

The extra assumption
\|\nabla_{v}g_{0}\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}
in (
8.50
) was introduced in
[
2
]
to establish local well-posedness.
This condition is automatically satisfied under the assumption (
1.19
) in Theorem
2
. In Proposition
21
, we impose the additional assumption (
8.50
) in order to apply the local well-posedness framework of
[
2
]
.

8.4.
Proof of Theorem
2
.

Since the assumptions in Proposition
21
are satisfied, there exists
T^{\prime}\ll 1
such that existence and uniqueness hold on
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
. Since
\|wg(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
is continuous in time by Proposition
21
, we obtain the continuity of
\|wf(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
in
t
. Hence, the assumptions in Proposition
19
are satisfied, and (
8.16
) yields

\|wf(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{2}\delta_{2}e^{-\lambda T^{\prime}}.

We choose
\delta_{3}
in Proposition
21
such that
C_{2}\delta_{2}<\delta_{3}
. Since the lifespan
T^{\prime}
of the local solution in Proposition
21
depends only on
\delta_{3}
, and the regularity estimate at
T^{\prime}
satisfies

\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{6}C_{5}.

Then we apply Proposition
21
with initial condition at
t=T^{\prime}
to obtain existence and uniqueness of the solution on
[T^{\prime},2T^{\prime}]
. From the well-posedness and continuity of
\|wf(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
on
[0,2T^{\prime}]
, Proposition
19
implies that

\displaystyle\|wf(2T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{2}\delta_{2}e^{-2\lambda T^{\prime}}.

Using (
8.51
), we control the regularity at
t=2T^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}g(2T^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(2T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g(2T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle<C_{6}[\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g(T^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}]<C_{7}|C_{6}|^{2}C_{5},

where we use
\|\nabla_{v}g\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}<C_{7}\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
from some constant
C_{7}>1
.
Inductively, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, existence and uniqueness hold on
[(n-1)T^{\prime},nT^{\prime}]
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\|wf(nT^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<C_{2}\delta_{2}e^{-\lambda nT^{\prime}},

(8.58)

\displaystyle\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}g(nT^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{g}\partial_{x,v}g(nT^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}g(nT^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<|C_{7}|^{n-1}|C_{6}|^{n}C_{5}.

This establishes the global well-posedness and the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
estimate (
1.20
).

Note that the regularity estimate (
8.58
) grows exponentially in time. To justify the uniform-in-time bound (
1.21
), we turn to the a priori estimate in Proposition
20
. The continuity of
g
established in Proposition
21
implies the continuity of
\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{f}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}},\|w_{\tilde{\theta}}\alpha_{f}\partial_{x,v}\mathfrak{f}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
.
Applying the continuity argument on
\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}
together with Lemma
19
, we obtain (
8.19
).
Hence, all assumptions in Proposition
20
are satisfied. Consequently, the uniform-in-time estimates (
8.20
) and (
1.21
) follow from Proposition
20
.

Finally, positivity holds for the local solution on
[0,T^{\prime}]
by Proposition
21
. By induction, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, positivity also holds on the interval
[(n-1)T^{\prime},nT^{\prime}]
. Therefore, the global solution satisfies
F(t,x,v)\geq 0
.
This, together with the asymptotic stability of the dynamical solution
F(t,x,v)
, implies the positivity of the stationary solution
F_{s}(x,v)\geq 0
in Theorem
1
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
2
.
∎
